thcal{S}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial_{U}\ell$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\partial_{U}\ell$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial_{U}\ell$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial_{U}\ell$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,V

Then we will write
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}}
for the dependent sum
\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{U:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{U:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{U:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{U:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}}}\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}\,U\,U
classifying
heaps
. We will write
\pi_{\mathscr{H}}:\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
for the first projection of a packed heap; given
H:\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}}
and
\ell:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
, we will write
H\mathbin{@}\ell:\mathord{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle X\leftarrow\partial_{\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H}}\ell$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle X\leftarrow\partial_{\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H}}\ell$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle X\leftarrow\partial_{\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H}}\ell$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X\leftarrow\partial_{\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H}}\ell$}\mathclose{}{]}}X\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
for the element stored by
H
at location
\ell
.

Presheaf categories and unenriched adjunctions

Let
\mathcal{S}
be a guarded universe closed under finite products.
As
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}}
is a 1-type, we can equally well view it as a category whose hom sets are given by identity types,
i.e
.
a groupoid. From this point of view, the projection
\pi_{\mathscr{H}}:\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}}\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}
extends to functors
\pi_{\mathscr{H}}:\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}}\to\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}
and
\bar{\pi}_{\mathscr{H}}:\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}}\cong\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}}^{\mathsf{op}}\to\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathsf{op}}
. We will use these projections to construct a network of adjunctions between the following presheaf categories:

\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}:\equiv\mathsf{Fun}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\bar{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathcal{S}}:\equiv\mathsf{Fun}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathsf{op}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathsf{op}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathsf{op}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathsf{op}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{S}}:\equiv\mathsf{Fun}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathsf{op}},\mathsf{0Dom}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathsf{op}},\mathsf{0Dom}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathsf{op}},\mathsf{0Dom}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathsf{op}},\mathsf{0Dom}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}:\equiv\mathsf{Fun}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

Exegesis 16
.

\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}
is the category on which our higher-order state monad is defined; this monad arises from a call-by-push-value adjunction
[
29
]
in which
\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}
is the category of “value types and pure functions” and
\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{S}}
is the category of “computation types and stacks”. A computation type differs from a value type in two ways, as it is both
contravariantly
indexed in worlds and valued in 0-domains rather than sets. We will treat these differences modularly by factoring the adjunction
\mathsf{F}\dashv\mathsf{U}:\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{S}}\to\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}
through further adjunctions. Our first adjoint resolution, to deal strictly with variance, is described in
\cref
lem:unenriched-adjoints; later on in
\cref
lem:enriched-lift, we will lift the adjunction between sets and 0-domains to the world of presheaves.

Lemma 17
.

Let
\mathcal{S}
be a small, set-reflective, guarded subuniverse of a guarded Martin-Löf universe
\mathcal{U}
containing
\mathsf{Inj}
. Then the unenriched base change functors
\Delta_{\pi_{\mathscr{H}}}:\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\to\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}
and
\Delta_{\bar{\pi}_{\mathscr{H}}}:\bar{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathcal{S}}\to\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}
has left and right adjoints
\exists_{\bar{\pi}_{\mathscr{H}}}\dashv\Delta_{\bar{\pi}_{\mathscr{H}}}
and
\Delta_{\pi_{\mathscr{H}}}\dashv\forall_{\pi_{\mathscr{H}}}
respectively.

Proof.

As
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}}
is both discrete and
\mathcal{U}
-small, the Kan extensions exist because
\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}
has all
\mathcal{U}
-small coproducts and products as a reflective subuniverse of
\mathcal{U}
.
∎

The unenriched adjunctions of
\cref
lem:unenriched-adjoints can be computed on objects as follows, where
\medcirc:\mathcal{U}\to\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}
is the assumed reflection:

\exists_{\bar{\pi}_{\mathscr{H}}}\,A\,U=\medcirc{\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{H:\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{H:\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{H:\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{H:\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}}}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{f:\hom_{\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathsf{op}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H},U$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H},U$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H},U$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H},U$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{f:\hom_{\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathsf{op}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H},U$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H},U$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H},U$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H},U$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{f:\hom_{\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathsf{op}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H},U$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H},U$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H},U$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H},U$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{f:\hom_{\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathsf{op}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H},U$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H},U$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H},U$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H},U$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}AH}

\forall_{\pi_{\mathscr{H}}}A\,U=\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{H:\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{H:\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{H:\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{H:\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}}}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{f:\hom_{\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle U,\pi_{\mathscr{H}}H$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle U,\pi_{\mathscr{H}}H$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle U,\pi_{\mathscr{H}}H$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle U,\pi_{\mathscr{H}}H$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\textstyle\prod_{f:\hom_{\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle U,\pi_{\mathscr{H}}H$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle U,\pi_{\mathscr{H}}H$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle U,\pi_{\mathscr{H}}H$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle U,\pi_{\mathscr{H}}H$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{f:\hom_{\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle U,\pi_{\mathscr{H}}H$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle U,\pi_{\mathscr{H}}H$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle U,\pi_{\mathscr{H}}H$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle U,\pi_{\mathscr{H}}H$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{f:\hom_{\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle U,\pi_{\mathscr{H}}H$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle U,\pi_{\mathscr{H}}H$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle U,\pi_{\mathscr{H}}H$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle U,\pi_{\mathscr{H}}H$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}AH

We draw attention to the fact that codomain of
{\exists}_{\bar{\pi}_{\mathscr{H}}}
is
{\bar{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathcal{S}}}

while the codomain of
{\forall}_{\bar{\pi}_{\mathscr{H}}}
is
{\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}}
, hence the
appearance of
\hom_{\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathsf{op}}}
in the former and
\hom_{\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}}
in the latter.

Enrichments and enriched adjunctions

Let
\mathcal{S}
be a guarded universe closed under finite products.
We now impose a common enrichment on
\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}},\bar{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathcal{S}},\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{S}},\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}
so as to lift
\cref
lem:unenriched-adjoints to the enriched level, making all these categories
locally indexed
in
\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}
in the sense of
[
29
]
. Given a co-presheaf
\Gamma\in\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}
, we will write
\pi_{\Gamma}:\tilde{\Gamma}\to\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathsf{op}}
for the discrete cartesian fibration corresponding to
\Gamma
. With this in hand, we impose the following additional notations:

\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\Gamma}:\equiv\mathsf{Fun}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{op}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{op}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{op}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{op}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\bar{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\Gamma}:\equiv\mathsf{Fun}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\Gamma},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\Gamma},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\Gamma},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\Gamma},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\Gamma}:\equiv\mathsf{Fun}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\Gamma},\mathsf{0Dom}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\Gamma},\mathsf{0Dom}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\Gamma},\mathsf{0Dom}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\Gamma},\mathsf{0Dom}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\Gamma}:\equiv\mathsf{Fun}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{op}}\times_{\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{op}}\times_{\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{op}}\times_{\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{op}}\times_{\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

Above, we note that
\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\Gamma}
is equivalent to the slice
\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}/\Gamma
; in the definition of
\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\Gamma}
, the expression
\tilde{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{op}}\times_{\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}}
refers to the pullback of the span
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{op}}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{op}}}\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}\xleftarrow{\pi_{\mathscr{H}}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{op}}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{op}}}\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}\xleftarrow{\pi_{\mathscr{H}}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{op}}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{op}}}\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}\xleftarrow{\pi_{\mathscr{H}}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{op}}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\Gamma}^{\mathsf{op}}}\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}\xleftarrow{\pi_{\mathscr{H}}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{S}}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
. We have the following base change functors for any co-presheaf
\Gamma\in\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}
:

\Delta_{\Gamma}:\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}\to\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\Gamma}

\Delta_{\Gamma}A\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle U,\gamma$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle U,\gamma$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle U,\gamma$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle U,\gamma$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\equiv AU

\Delta_{\Gamma}:\bar{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathcal{S}}\to\bar{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\Gamma}

\Delta_{\Gamma}X\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle U,\gamma$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle U,\gamma$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle U,\gamma$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle U,\gamma$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\equiv XU

\Delta_{\Gamma}:\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{S}}\to\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\Gamma}

\Delta_{\Gamma}\,X\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle U,\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle U,\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle U,\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle U,\Gamma$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\equiv XU

\Delta_{\Gamma}:\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}\to\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\Gamma}

\Delta_{\Gamma}\,A\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle U,\Gamma,H$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle U,\Gamma,H$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle U,\Gamma,H$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle U,\Gamma,H$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\equiv A\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle U,H$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle U,H$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle U,H$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle U,H$}\mathclose{}{)}}

Construction 18

(Enrichments)
.

We extend
\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}
,
\bar{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathcal{S}}
,
\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{S}}
, and
\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}
to
\hat{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathcal{S}}
-enriched categories
\underline{\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}}
,
\underline{\bar{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathcal{S}}}
,
\underline{\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{S}}}
, and
\underline{\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}}
respectively, regarding
\hat{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathcal{S}}
with its
cartesian
monoidal structure:

\hom_{\underline{\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{\Gamma}:\equiv\hom_{\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\Gamma}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta_{\Gamma}A,\Delta_{\Gamma}B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\Delta_{\Gamma}A,\Delta_{\Gamma}B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta_{\Gamma}A,\Delta_{\Gamma}B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta_{\Gamma}A,\Delta_{\Gamma}B$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\hom_{\underline{\bar{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathcal{S}}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle X,Y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle X,Y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle X,Y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X,Y$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{\Gamma}:\equiv\hom_{\bar{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\Gamma}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta_{\Gamma}X,\Delta_{\Gamma}Y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\Delta_{\Gamma}X,\Delta_{\Gamma}Y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta_{\Gamma}X,\Delta_{\Gamma}Y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta_{\Gamma}X,\Delta_{\Gamma}Y$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\hom_{\underline{\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{S}}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle X,Y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle X,Y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle X,Y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X,Y$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{\Gamma}:\equiv\hom_{\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\Gamma}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta_{\Gamma}X,\Delta_{\Gamma}Y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\Delta_{\Gamma}X,\Delta_{\Gamma}Y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta_{\Gamma}X,\Delta_{\Gamma}Y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta_{\Gamma}X,\Delta_{\Gamma}Y$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\hom_{\underline{\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A,B$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{\Gamma}:\equiv\hom_{\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\Gamma}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Delta_{\Gamma}A,\Delta_{\Gamma}B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\Delta_{\Gamma}A,\Delta_{\Gamma}B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta_{\Gamma}A,\Delta_{\Gamma}B$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta_{\Gamma}A,\Delta_{\Gamma}B$}\mathclose{}{)}}

These enrichments agree with those given by Levy
[
29
]
in terms of dinatural transformations as one can see using the formula for a natural transformation as an end. The purpose of imposing these enrichments was to be able to state
\cref
lem:enriched-adjoints,lem:enriched-lift below.

Lemma 19
.

Under the assumptions of
\cref
lem:unenriched-adjoints, the unenriched adjunctions
\Delta_{\pi_{\mathscr{H}}}\dashv\forall_{\pi_{\mathscr{H}}}
and
\exists_{\bar{\pi}_{\mathscr{H}}}\dashv\Delta_{\bar{\pi}_{\mathscr{H}}}
extend to
\hat{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathcal{S}}
-enriched adjunctions

\underline{\Delta}_{\pi_{\mathscr{H}}}\dashv\underline{\forall}_{\pi_{\mathscr{H}}}
and
\underline{\exists}_{\bar{\pi}_{\mathscr{H}}}\dashv\underline{\Delta}_{\bar{\pi}_{\mathscr{H}}}
.

Lemma 20
.

Let
\mathcal{S}
be a guarded universe closed under binary coproducts. Then the adjunction
\mathsf{L}\dashv\mathsf{R}:\mathsf{0Dom}_{\mathcal{S}}\to\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}
between sets and guarded 0-domains (
\cref
lem:dom-set-adjunction) can be lifted pointwise to an enriched adjunction
\mathsf{L}{}\dashv\mathsf{R}{}:\underline{\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{S}}}\to\underline{\bar{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathcal{S}}}
.

The call-by-push-value adjunction and resulting strong monad

Let
\mathcal{S}
be a small, set-reflective, guarded subuniverse of a guarded Martin-Löf universe
\mathcal{U}
containing
\mathsf{Inj}
.
We can compose the enriched adjunctions obtained in

\cref
lem:enriched-adjoints to obtain a single enriched adjunction

\mathsf{F}{}\dashv\mathsf{U}{}:\underline{\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{S}}}\to\underline{\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}}
, setting

\mathsf{F}{}:\equiv\mathsf{L}{}\circ\underline{\exists}_{\bar{\pi}_{\mathscr{H}}}\circ\underline{\Delta}_{\pi_{\mathscr{H}}}
and

\mathsf{U}{}:\equiv\underline{\forall}_{\pi_{\mathscr{H}}}\circ\underline{\Delta}_{\bar{\pi}_{\mathscr{H}}}\circ\mathsf{R}{}
as
depicted in
\cref
fig:cbpv-adjunction. We will write
\mathsf{ret}
for the unit of this
adjunction. Our adjunction is an adjoint decomposition of Levy’s possible worlds model of
general storage
[
29
]
, with Levy’s syntactic Kripke worlds replaced by

recursively defined univalent semantic worlds
, as can be seen from
\cref
cmp:adjoints
below.

\underline{\bar{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathcal{S}}}
\underline{\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}}
\underline{\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}}
\underline{\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{S}}}
\underline{\Delta}_{\bar{\pi}_{\mathscr{H}}}
\underline{\forall}_{\pi_{\mathscr{H}}}
\underline{\Delta}_{\pi_{\mathscr{H}}}
\underline{\exists}_{\bar{\pi}_{\mathscr{H}}}
\mathsf{R}{}
\mathsf{L}{}
\bot
\bot
\bot
\mathsf{U}{}
\mathsf{F}{}

Figure 4:
A diagram of
\hat{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathcal{S}}
-enriched adjunctions, together comprising a call-by-push-value adjunction
\mathsf{F}{}\dashv\mathsf{U}{}:\underline{\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{S}}}\to\underline{\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}}
resolving an enriched (and thus strong) monad
\mathsf{IO}=\mathsf{U}\circ\mathsf{F}
on
\underline{\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}}
.

Lemma 22
.

Under the assumptions of
\cref
lem:later-in-presheaves, each
\mathsf{U}{X}
is a guarded domain.

3.2.3
The model of univalent reference types

We have now defined all the basic elements of our model.

General recursion and stepping

Let
\mathcal{S}
be a small, set-reflective, guarded subuniverse of a guarded Martin-Löf universe
\mathcal{U}
containing
\mathsf{Inj}
.
Abstract steps in our model are encoded in terms of a global element
\mathsf{step}:\mathsf{IO}\,{\mathbf{1}}
, defined using the guarded domain structure of
\mathsf{IO}\,\mathbf{1}
as
\mathsf{step}:\equiv\vartheta_{\mathsf{IO}\,\mathbf{1}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{next}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{ret}\,{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{ret}\,{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{ret}\,{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{ret}\,{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{next}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{ret}\,{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{ret}\,{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{ret}\,{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{ret}\,{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{next}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{ret}\,{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{ret}\,{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{ret}\,{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{ret}\,{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{next}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{ret}\,{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{ret}\,{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{ret}\,{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{ret}\,{*}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

Lemma 23
.

For any
u:\mathsf{IO}\,{A}
, we have
\mathsf{step};u=\vartheta_{\mathsf{IO}\,A}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{next}\,u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{next}\,u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{next}\,u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{next}\,u$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

Proof.

By unfolding the definition of the
\mathord{\blacktriangleright}
-algebra structure pointwise in the model.
∎

Using
\mathsf{fix}_{\mathord{\blacktriangleright}}
, we can define a monadic fixed point combinator satisfying the equation
\mathsf{rec}\,h\,a=\mathsf{step};h\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{rec}\,h$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{rec}\,h$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{rec}\,h$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{rec}\,h$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,a
.

Store operations: getting, setting, and allocation

Let
\mathcal{S}
be a small,
\Sigma
-closed, set-reflective, guarded subuniverse of a guarded Martin-Löf universe
\mathcal{U}
containing
\mathsf{Inj}
. In this section, we explicitly construct the store operations in
\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}
, which we summarize in
\cref
fig:store-operations.

\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}

Construction 25

(Detailed construction of the getter)
.

For any
A\in\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}
, we can interpret the getter as a natural transformation
\mathsf{get}:\mathsf{IORef}\,{A}\to\mathsf{IO}\,A
in
\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}
. Because the definition is a little subtle, we will do it step-by-step.

Construction 26

(Detailed construction of the setter)
.

For each
A\in\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}
, we define the setter as a natural transformation
\mathsf{IORef}\,A\times A\to\mathsf{IO}\,\mathbf{1}
in
\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}
.

\mathsf{set}:\mathsf{IORef}\,A\times A\to\mathsf{IO}\,\mathbf{1}

\mathsf{set}_{U}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\ell,\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\ell,\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\ell,\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\ell,\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}},a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\ell,\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\ell,\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\ell,\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\ell,\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}},a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\ell,\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\ell,\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\ell,\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\ell,\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}},a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\ell,\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\ell,\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\ell,\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\ell,\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}},a$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,H\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle H
at the location
{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle f$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle f$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}\,\ell}

\mathsf{next}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle Af\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle Af\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle Af\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Af\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}:\mathord{\blacktriangleright}{{A\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}}
; for this to make sense, we need to transport along the (delayed) identification

Above, we have defined a new heap
H_{a}
whose underlying finite set of locations is the coproduct
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle{\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\pi_{\mathscr{H}}{H}}$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}+\mathbf{1}
, filling the new location with
a
and return the pointer to this location.

The main theorem

We now come to the main result of this paper, which obtains a model of univalent reference types from a suitably structured small set-reflective subuniverse.

Theorem 28
.

Let
\mathcal{S}
be a small,
\Sigma
-closed, set-reflective, guarded subuniverse of a guarded Martin-Löf universe
\mathcal{U}
containing
\mathsf{Inj}
such that
\mathcal{S}
is additionally closed under the type of natural numbers. Then there is a model of the monadic language from
\cref
sec:language satisfying the equational theory of univalent reference types (
\cref
fig:rules,fig:univalent), in which:

1.

contexts, types, and terms are interpreted in the category
\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}=\mathsf{Fun}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
;

2.

the reference type connective is interpreted as in
\cref
con:ref-copresheaf;

3.

the computational monad is given by
\mathsf{IO}=\mathsf{U}\circ\mathsf{F}
as defined in
\cref
fig:cbpv-adjunction;

4.

general recursion and the store operations are interpreted as in
\cref
sec:model:recursion,sec:model:store.

Proof.

We note that
\mathscr{P}_{\mathcal{S}}=\mathsf{Fun}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}},\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is locally cartesian closed in spite of the fact that
\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}
is as large as
\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}
is: local cartesian closure nonetheless follows because
\mathsf{Set}_{\mathcal{S}}
is reflective in
\mathcal{U}
and
\mathscr{C}_{\mathcal{S}}
is
\mathcal{U}
-small.
Everything except the two laws of
univalent
reference types (
\cref
fig:univalent) follows in the same way as in the non-univalent model given by Sterling
et al
.

[
43
]
. The
allocation permutation
law holds under the interpretations given because the two heaps resulting from allocations in different orders are identified under univalence. The
representation independence
law holds for similar reasons, considering the effect of
transporting
along an identification between equivalent heaps on the getter and the setter.
∎

4
Models of guarded HoTT with impredicative universes

Our main result (
\cref
thm:the-model) is contingent on there existing a model of guarded homotopy type theory in which there can be found a suitably small,
\Sigma
-closed, set-reflective, guarded subuniverse of a guarded Martin-Löf universe containing
\mathsf{Inj}
. It is by no means obvious that such a model exists, but in this section we will provide some preliminary evidence.

1.

Sterling, Gratzer, and Birkedal
[
43
]
have constructed models of
impredicative guarded dependent type theory
(
iGDTT
), a non-univalent version of our metalanguage.

2.

Awodey
[
11
]
has constructed a model of impredicative homotopy type theory in
cubical assemblies
,
i.e
.
internal cubical sets in the category of assemblies. Uemura
[
45
]
subsequently described a variant of this model in the style of Orton and Pitts
[
34
]
.

3.

Birkedal
et al
.

[
15
,
14
]
have constructed an Orton–Pitts model of
guarded cubical type theory
in presheaves on the product of a cube category with the ordinal
\omega
. This model was revisited in the context of multi-modal type theory by Aagaard
et al
.

[
1
]
.

The methods of the papers above are essentially modular, and are furthermore not particularly sensitive to the choice of cube category or ordinal, so long as these can be defined in assemblies without resorting to quotients.

Conjecture 29

(Soundness)
.

There is a non-trivial model of guarded homotopy type theory in guarded cubical assemblies in which there is a small, set-reflective, guarded Martin-Löf subuniverse
\mathcal{S}\subseteq\mathcal{U}
of a guarded Martin-Löf universe
\mathcal{U}
containing
\mathsf{Inj}
.

5
Conclusions and future work

We have demonstrated the impact of a univalent metalanguage on the denotational semantics of higher-order store, extending the guarded global allocation model of Sterling
et al
.

[
43
]
with new program equivalences: invariance under permutation and representation independence in the heap. We believe that we have only scratched the surface of the potential for univalent denotational semantics in general, and univalent reference types in particular; we describe a few potential areas for further development beyond substantiating
\cref
conj:soundness.

1.

Sterling
et al
.

[
43
]
have given non-univalent denotational semantics of
polymorphic

\lambda
-calculus with recursive types and general reference types. It is within reach to adapt this model to the univalent setting, obtaining even more program equivalences than before. In particular, many data abstraction theorems for existential packages that typically hold only up to observational equivalence are expected to hold on the nose.

2.

Our case study, an equation between two object-oriented counters, involves invariance of the heap under
isomorphisms
between data representations — whereas parametricity is often employed in cases of correspondences that are not isomorphisms. Angiuli
et al
.

[
7
]
have shown that many such applications of parametricity are nonetheless subsumed by univalence in the presence of quotient types, and thus many more observational equivalences can be replaced with honest equations in univalent denotational semantics. We are eager to put the wisdom of
op. cit
.
into practice in the context of imperative and object-oriented programming by incorporating quotients into our theory and model.

3.

Although our theory validates many more desirable equations than the global store theory of Sterling
et al
.

[
43
]
, we do not come close to modeling full local store: for example, two programs that allocate different numbers of cells cannot be equal. We hope that it will be possible to adapt the methods of Kammar
et al
.

[
27
]
to the guarded, univalent, and impredicative setting in order to develop even more abstract models of mutable state.

4.

Our language does not allow for references to be directly compared (
nominal
references
) and no such equality testing function exists in our model. Prior
work
[
44
,
33
]
has given models of such references using
the theory of nominal sets
[
23
,
37
]
. We hope that these methods may
be adapted to our model in order to support nominal univalent references.
